The only variable left is L, which must be a positive integer such that both L and R are integers. content: {"reasoning":"To solve the equation $z^5 = \bar{z}$, we can express $z$ in polar form as $z = re^{i\theta}$, where $r$ is the modulus and $\theta$ is the argument of the complex number. The conjugate of $z$ is then $\bar{z} = re^{-i\theta}$. Therefore, the equation becomes: \n\n$$ (re^{i\theta})^5 = re^{-i\theta} $$\n\nThis simplifies to:\n\n$$ r^5 e^{5i\theta} = re^{-i\theta}. $$\n\nDividing both sides by $r$ (assuming $r \neq 0$), we get:\n\n$$ r^4 e^{5i\theta} = e^{-i\theta}. $$\n\nThis leads us to:\n\n$$ r^4 = 1 \text{ and } e^{5i\theta} = e^{-i\theta}. $$\n\nFrom $r^4 = 1$, we find that $r = 1$ (since $r$ is non-negative). Thus, $z$ lies on the unit circle. \n\nNext, from $e^{5i\theta} = e^{-i\theta}$, we can equate the arguments:\n\n$$ 5\theta = -\theta + 2k\theta \text{ for } k \text{ an integer.} $$\n\nThis simplifies to:\n\n$$ 6\theta = 2k\theta $$\n\nwhich implies:\n\n$$ \theta(6 - 2k) = 0. $$\n\nThis gives us two cases:\n1. $\theta = 0$, which gives one solution: $z = 1$.\n2. $6 - 2k = 0$, which gives $k = 3$. content: {
  "reasoning": "To find the number of complex numbers that satisfy the equation z^5 = \bar{z}, we can rewrite this equation using polar coordinates. Let z = re^(iθ), where r is the modulus and θ is the argument of the complex number. The conjugate of z can be expressed as \bar{z} = re^(-iθ). Substituting these into the equation gives us (re^(iθ))^5 = re^(-iθ), which simplifies to r^5e^(5iθ) = re^(-iθ). Dividing both sides by r (assuming r ≠ 0), we have r^4e^(5iθ) = e^(-iθ). This leads us to the equality r^4 = 1 (thus r = 1) and 5θ = -θ + 2kπ for some integer k, which simplifies to 6θ = 2kπ or θ = (kπ)/3. content: {"reasoning":"To find the probability that Flora the frog will eventually land at 10, we need to consider the nature of her jumps. The probability of jumping a distance m is given by \\( P(m) = \\frac{1}{2^m} \\). The total probability of making any jump is the sum of the probabilities for all possible jumps: \\( P = \\sum_{m=1}^{\\infty} \\frac{1}{2^m} = 1 \\). This indicates that Flora will eventually make an infinite number of jumps with probabilities summing to 1. To calculate the probability of specifically landing on 10, we can use the fact that the expected number of ways to reach 10 is equal to the probability of all possible combinations of jumps that sum to 10. 문제 {total}:  If $A$ and $B$ are vertices of a polyhedron, define the distance $d(A,B)$ to be the minimum number of edges of the polyhedron one must traverse in order to connect $A$ and $B$. For example, if $\overline{AB}$ is an edge of the polyhedron, then $d(A, B) = 1$, but if $\overline{AC}$ and $\overline{CB}$ are edges and $\overline{AB}$ is not an edge, then $d(A, B) = 2$. Let $Q$, $R$, and $S$ be randomly chosen distinct vertices of a regular icosahedron (regular polyhedron made up of 20 equilateral triangles). Find the probability that $d(Q, R) > d(R, S)$. The final answer can be written in the form $\frac{m}{n}$, where $m$ and $n$ are relatively prime positive integers. What is $m+n$?

문제 {total}:  Janet rolls a standard $6$-sided die $4$ times and keeps a running total of the numbers she rolls. What is the probability that at some point, her running total will equal $3$? The final answer can be written in the form $\frac{m}{n}$, where $m$ and $n$ are relatively prime positive integers. What is $m+n$?
모델 답변: 1
실제 정답: 265
--------------------------------------------------
content: {
  "reasoning": "Let the coordinates of points A and B be (x1, log2(x1)) and (x2, log2(x2)) respectively. The midpoint of AB is given by ( (x1 + x2)/2, (log2(x1) + log2(x2))/2 ). Since the midpoint is (6, 2), we have two equations: (x1 + x2)/2 = 6, which simplifies to x1 + x2 = 12, and (log2(x1) + log2(x2))/2 = 2, which simplifies to log2(x1) + log2(x2) = 4. Using properties of logarithms, we can express the second equation as log2(x1 * x2) = 4, leading to x1 * x2 = 16. We now have a system of equations: x1 + x2 = 12 and x1 * x2 = 16. We can solve this system by substituting x2 = 12 - x1 into the second equation, resulting in the quadratic equation x1^2 - 12x1 + 16 = 0. Expressing this in the form m√n, we have m = 4 and n = 2, which are relatively prime. Thus, m + n = 4 + 2 = 6.",
  "answer": 6

content: {
  "reasoning": "Let the current mean of Maureen's quiz scores be M, and let n be the number of quizzes she has taken so far. The total score for these quizzes is n * M. If she scores 11 on the next quiz, her new total score will be n * M + 11, and the number of quizzes will increase to n + 1. Therefore, the new mean after one quiz will be (n * M + 11) / (n + 1). The problem states that this new mean will be M + 1. We can set up the equation: (n * M + 11) / (n + 1) = M + 1. Cross-multiplying gives us n * M + 11 = (M + 1) * (n + 1) which simplifies to n * M + 11 = n * M + M + n + 1. This leads to the equation 11 = M + n + 1, or M + n = 10. Now, if Maureen scores 11 on each of the next three quizzes, her new total score will be n * M + 33 (since 11 * 3 = 33), and the number of quizzes will be n + 3. The new mean will therefore be (n * M + 33) / (n + 3). This new mean is stated to be M + 2. Setting up the equation: (n * M + 33) / (n + 3) = M + 2. Cross-multiplying gives us n * M + 33 = (M + 2) * (n + 3), which simplifies to n * M + 33 = n * M + 2n + 3M + 6. This leads to 33 = 2n + 3M + 6, or 2n + 3M = 27. Now we have a system of two equations: M + n = 10 and 2n + 3M = 27. We can solve for n from the first equation: n = 10 - M. Substituting this into the second equation gives us 2(10 - M) + 3M = 27, leading to 20 - 2M + 3M = 27, or M = 7. Thus, the current mean of Maureen's quiz scores is 7.",
  "answer": 7
}
content: {
  "reasoning": "Let M be the current mean of Maureen's quiz scores and N be the number of quizzes she has taken. The total score from her quizzes is N * M. 문제 {total}:  Mrs. Jones is pouring orange juice into four identical glasses for her four sons. She fills the first three glasses completely but runs out of juice when the fourth glass is only $\frac{1}{3}$ full. What fraction of a glass must Mrs. Jones pour from each of the first three glasses into the fourth glass so that all four glasses will have the same amount of juice? The final answer can be written in the form $\frac{m}{n}$, where $m$ and $n$ are relatively prime positive integers. What is $m+n$?

실제 정답: 7

content: {"reasoning":"Let's denote the first circle with radius 4 and center on the positive x-axis. Since it is tangent to the y-axis at the origin, its center must be at (4, 0). The equation of this circle is: (x - 4)² + y² = 4², which simplifies to (x - 4)² + y² = 16. \n\nNow, for the second circle with radius 10 and center on the positive y-axis, since it is tangent to the x-axis at the origin, its center must be at (0, 10). The equation of this circle is: x² + (y - 10)² = 10², which simplifies to x² + (y - 10)² = 100.\n\nTo find the intersection points, we will solve these two equations:\n1. (x - 4)² + y² = 16\n2. x² + (y - 10)² = 100\n\nExpanding both equations, we get:\n1. x² - 8x + 16 + y² = 16  =>  x² - 8x + y² = 0\n2. x² + y² - 20y + 100 = 100 => x² + y² - 20y = 0\n\nNow we can subtract the first equation from the second:\n(x² + y² - 20y) - (x² - 8x + y²) = 0,\nwhich simplifies to 8x - 20y = 0 or 8x = 20y, leading to y = 0.4x.\n\nTo find the intersection points, substitute y = 0.4x into either original equation, let's use the first:\n(x - 4)² + (0.4x)² = 16. \nExpanding this gives:\n(x - 4)² + 0.16x² = 16,\nwhich leads to:\nx² - 8x + 16 + 0.16x² = 16,\n1.16x² - 8x = 0,\nx(1.16x - 8) = 0.\nThus, x = 0 or x = 8/1.16 = 69/29. \n\nIf x = 0, then y = 0. If x = 69/29, then y = 0.4 * (69/29) = 27.6/29.\n\nThe intersection points are (0, 0) and (69/29, 27.6/29).\n\nTo find the slope of the line between these two points, we can use the formula (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1):\nSlope = (27.6/29 - 0) / (69/29 - 0) = (27.6/29) / (69/29) = 27.6 / 69 = 0.4.\n\nIn fractional form, 0.4 = 2/5. Here, m = 2 and n = 5, which are relatively prime. Therefore, m + n = 2 + 5 = 7.","answer":7}

문제 {total}:  In the $xy$-plane, a circle of radius $4$ with center on the positive $x$-axis is tangent to the $y$-axis at the origin, and a circle with radius $10$ with center on the positive $y$-axis is tangent to the $x$-axis at the origin. What is the slope of the line passing through the two points at which these circles intersect? The final answer can be written in the form $\frac{m}{n}$, where $m$ and $n$ are relatively prime positive integers. What is $m+n$?

To find the maximum area, we need to calculate the height of the trapezoid in terms of a. By dropping perpendiculars from the endpoints of the shorter base to the longer base, we form a rectangle and two right triangles. The distance between the two perpendiculars is (b - a) / 2 = (2a - a) / 2 = a/2. Using the Pythagorean theorem, we can find the height (h) of the trapezoid as h = sqrt(1^2 - (a/2)^2) = sqrt(1 - a^2/4). The area (A) of the trapezoid can be expressed as A = (1/2) * (a + b) * h = (1/2) * (a + 2a) * sqrt(1 - a^2/4) = (3a/2) * sqrt(1 - a^2/4).   "reasoning": "Let the lengths of the bases of the isosceles trapezoid be denoted as 'b' for the shorter base and '2b' for the longer base. The legs of the trapezoid are both equal to 1. We can drop perpendiculars from the endpoints of the shorter base to the longer base, splitting the trapezoid into a rectangle and two right triangles. The height of the trapezoid can be denoted as 'h'. The trapezoid's area can be expressed as A = (1/2) * (b + 2b) * h = (3b/2) * h. To find the height, we can use the Pythagorean theorem on one of the right triangles formed. The horizontal distance from the ends of the shorter base to the base of the right triangle is (2b - b)/2 = b/2. Thus, we have h^2 + (b/2)^2 = 1^2, which gives us h = √(1 - (b/2)²). The area can then be expressed as A(b) = (3b/2) * √(1 - (b/2)²). To find the maximum area, we differentiate A(b) with respect to b, set the derivative to zero, and solve for b. This yields b = 2√(2)/3. Substituting b back into the area formula gives us the maximum area, which simplifies to 2√(3)/3. To express the area in the form m/n, we rationalize it to get m = 2√(3) and n = 3. Since these two are not coprime integers, we adjust to find m = 2 and n = 3. Thus, m^2 + n^2 = 2^2 + 3^2 = 4 + 9 = 13.",

content: {"reasoning":"Let the shorter base of the isosceles trapezoid be denoted as b. Then, the longer base will be 2b. The length of the legs (which are equal) is given as 1. The height (h) of the trapezoid can be determined using the Pythagorean theorem. Drawing the height from the vertices of the shorter base to the longer base, we form two right triangles. The horizontal component of each leg can be found as follows: the distance from the midpoint of the shorter base to the endpoint of the longer base is (2b-b)/2 = b/2. Therefore, we can set up the equation: (1)^2 = h^2 + (b/2)^2. This simplifies to h^2 = 1 - (b^2/4). The area (A) of the trapezoid can be expressed as A = (1/2) * (base1 + base2) * height = (1/2) * (b + 2b) * h = (3/2) * b * h. Substituting h gives us: A = (3/2) * b * sqrt(1 - (b^2/4). To maximize the area A, we take the derivative with respect to b, set it to zero, and solve for b. After finding the critical point and verifying it gives a maximum area, we can compute the maximum area. The maximum area is eventually found to be 3/4. As such, m = 3 and n = 4, both of which are relatively prime. Thus, m^2 + n^2 = 3^2 + 4^2 = 9 + 16 = 25.","answer":25}

문제 {total}:  For complex number $u = a+bi$ and $v = c+di$ (where $i=\sqrt{-1}$), define the binary operation
$u \otimes v = ac + bdi$
Suppose $z$ is a complex number such that $z\otimes z = z^{2}+40$. What is $|z|^2$?

Since 9 and 4 are relatively prime, we find m + n = 9 + 4 = 13.",

content: {
  "reasoning": "Let the edge lengths of the rectangular box be denoted as a, b, and c. The sum of the lengths of all 12 edges is given by the equation: 4(a + b + c) = 13, which simplifies to a + b + c = 13/4. The areas of all 6 faces can be represented as: ab + ac + bc = 11/2. The volume of the box is given by: abc = 1/2. To find the length of the longest interior diagonal connecting two vertices of the box, we use the formula for the diagonal length: d = sqrt(a^2 + b^2 + c^2). We need to express a^2 + b^2 + c^2 in terms of a + b + c, ab + ac + bc, and abc. This is done through the identity: (a + b + c)^2 = a^2 + b^2 + c^2 + 2(ab + ac + bc). Plugging in our values, we get: (13/4)^2 = a^2 + b^2 + c^2 + 2(11/2). Calculating this gives us: 169/16 = a^2 + b^2 + c^2 + 11. 문제 {total}:  Suppose that $a$, $b$, $c$ and $d$ are positive integers satisfying all of the following relations.
\[abcd=2^6\cdot 3^9\cdot 5^7\]
\[\text{lcm}(a,b)=2^3\cdot 3^2\cdot 5^3\]
\[\text{lcm}(a,c)=2^3\cdot 3^3\cdot 5^3\]
\[\text{lcm}(a,d)=2^3\cdot 3^3\cdot 5^3\]
\[\text{lcm}(b,c)=2^1\cdot 3^3\cdot 5^2\]
\[\text{lcm}(b,d)=2^2\cdot 3^3\cdot 5^2\]
\[\text{lcm}(c,d)=2^2\cdot 3^3\cdot 5^2\]
What is $\text{gcd}(a,b,c,d)$?

content: {"reasoning":"To find the ratio of the area of circle A to the area of circle B, we first need to determine the radii of the circles inscribed around the triangles. \n\nFor triangle A, which is a 3-4-5 right triangle, the hypotenuse (which is the diameter of the circle) is 5. The radius (rA) of circle A is half the hypotenuse: \n\\( r_A = \\frac{5}{2} \\).\n\nThe area of circle A can be calculated as: \n\\( A_A = \\pi r_A^2 = \\pi \\left(\\frac{5}{2}\\right)^2 = \\pi \\cdot \\frac{25}{4} = \\frac{25\\pi}{4} \\).\n\nFor triangle B, which is a 5-12-13 right triangle, the hypotenuse is 13.   "reasoning": "To find the ratio of the area of circle A to the area of circle B, we first need to determine the radii of the circles that circumscribe the triangles. The circumradius R of a right triangle with legs a and b, and hypotenuse c, can be calculated using the formula R = c/2, where c is the length of the hypotenuse. For triangle A (3-4-5), the hypotenuse is 5, so the circumradius R_A = 5/2 = 2.5. The area of circle A is then π(R_A^2) = π(2.5^2) = π(6.25). For triangle B (5-12-13), the hypotenuse is 13, so the circumradius R_B = 13/2 = 6.5. The area of circle B is then π(R_B^2) = π(6.5^2) = π(42.25). The ratio of the areas of the two circles is (Area of circle A) / (Area of circle B) = (π(6.25)) / (π(42.25)) = 6.25 / 42.25. This simplifies to (6.25 × 4) / (42.25 × 4) = 25 / 169. Since 25 and 169 have no common factors, they are relatively prime. Therefore, m = 25 and n = 169. The final answer is m + n = 25 + 169 = 194.",

The numerator can be simplified: \\( \\log(n^2) = 2\\log(n) \\), so we have \\( 2\\log(n) - (\\log n)^2 = \\log(n)(2 - \\log(n)) \\).
